To what extent does the company share its scale economies with customers, stated or de facto, in its messaging, strategy, culture or values?

F4222nd of 32

Layer 1 · Company - Competitive advantage

The Investment Analyst's heading To what extent does the company share its scale economies with customers, stated or de facto?

Judgement: Convatec does not share scale economies as a strategy; its scale gains (G&A, automation) are retained in margin, and what it shares is service and education rather than price. That is consistent with a margin-recovery story and with an industry where list price is not the competitive weapon, but it means scale does not compound into share the way a Costco-type model does.

  • Shared de facto: me+ patient programmes and the me+ Companion app; training of over 230,000 professionals; 180 Medical service levels; free or low-cost products for hospital placements (industry practice).
  • Retained: G&A savings from 13 to about 6 percent of sales and automation savings flow to the margin target.
C-47

To what extent do gravitational or mechanical forces, rather than heroic company dynamism, improve the company's competitive advantage and its financial outputs against peers with each period that passes?

C6012th of 32

Layer 1 · Company - Competitive advantage

Judgement: there are real mechanical forces, but they are of the "holding and accumulating" kind rather than the "winner takes more" kind: each discharge win accrues for a decade, each automated line lowers unit cost, each pump or drug that specifies the set adds contracted volume, and two accounting and base effects arrive mechanically. Against them, price erodes about 1 percent a year and the payer resets categories. Net, the financial outputs improve mechanically by perhaps one to two points of EPS growth a year beyond what management must win.

  • Mechanical positives: the discharge annuity (GPO access converts slowly into a growing community base); automation (unit cost falls as each plant converts); Infusion partners' own marketing of Vyalev, Onapgo and insulin pumps drives Convatec's set volume; supplier consolidation under bidding moves volume to 180 Medical; InnovaMatrix leaving the base (FY2027); amortisation roll-off (reported earnings from 2027); share-count reduction under the 2.0 times leverage policy.
  • Mechanical negatives: about 1 percent annual price erosion; payer resets; Coloplast's larger acute installed base pulls share its way in ostomy; input hedges roll off annually.
  • Peers: Coloplast's gravitational pull is stronger in ostomy and continence; Convatec's is stronger in infusion and US home delivery.

  • Incentive design: performance shares vest 50 percent on EPS growth, 25 percent on organic growth and 25 percent on relative TSR; the bonus weights adjusted operating profit 40 percent and cash flow 15 percent. These point management at exactly the EPS and margin lines the model forecasts.

Convatec's economics are solid and improving, not outstanding. It grows EPS at 13 to 16 percent on revenue growth of 6 to 7 percent, with a 22 percent operating margin rising towards 24 to 26 percent through overhead efficiency, automation and mix, moderate and comfortable leverage that funds a systematic buyback, and good underlying cash conversion of about 0.8 times adjusted earnings. FactSet's own gross margin line (54.4 percent in FY2025) includes amortisation in cost of sales and is not comparable. Redburn forecasts 58.7 percent by FY2027 on its own basis.
  • Adjusted operating margin: 17.7 percent (2021 trough after reinvestment), 19.5 (2022), 20.2 (2023), 21.2 (2024), 22.3 (2025), 21.2 (H1 2026); guided at least 23.0 percent (FY2026) and 24 to 26 percent (2027); consensus 23.0, 24.0, 24.6, 25.0 percent for FY2026 to FY2029.
  • EBITDA margin (brokers' basis): 26.5 percent (FY2025) rising to 27.3, 28.3, 29.0 and 29.4 percent.
  • Peers: Coloplast adjusted EBIT 25.7 percent (FY2025/26) now guided mid-20s; Smith & Nephew trading margin 19.7 percent; Mölnlycke EBIT about 24 percent.
C-54

How clean is the earnings base: how large is the gap between adjusted and reported earnings; do one-offs, capitalised costs or other items flatter it; and does the tax rate or any other non-obvious leakage erode earnings per share?

C-4816th of 32

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

The Investment Analyst's heading How clean is the earnings base: how large is the gap between adjusted and reported earnings; do one-offs, capitalised costs or other items flatter it; and does the tax rate or any other non-obvious leakage erode EPS?

Judgement: the earnings base is moderately clean on its adjusted definition and visibly unclean on the reported one, with a gap of about half; most of the gap is a legacy amortisation that ends in Dec 2026, but recurring impairments and a cash-flow redefinition show a tendency to present favourably. Tax and minority leakage are low.

  • Size of the gap: FY2025 reported net income $175m against adjusted earnings of about $360m (reported basic EPS 8.6 cents against adjusted 17.6 cents).
  • Items: amortisation of acquired intangibles from the 2008 spin-out (about 80 percent of the historical gap; about $93m to $95m a year; ending Dec 2026); impairments ($72m in FY2025; $69m in H1 2026, including a $21m expected credit loss on InnovaMatrix receivables, which is closer to an operating loss than a one-off); restructuring charges; a realised $32m loss on financing currency derivatives in 2025.
  • Capitalised costs: intangible capital expenditure of about $48m in FY2025 (total $183m against $135m fixed); the research does not say how much development cost is capitalised. The redefinition of free cash flow to equity removes $121m of growth capital expenditure from the headline cash measure.
  • Leakage: adjusted book tax rate 24 percent (cash rate lower); no minorities; net interest $71m.
  • Forward: from FY2027 reported EPS should converge strongly on adjusted, which should help reported-earnings screens and the quality perception; the Consumer should still adjust for recurring "adjusting items".
C-76

How material is the financial leverage or gearing: how much does it amplify earnings per share and risk, and how comfortable is it?

D6017th of 32

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

The Investment Analyst's heading How material is the financial leverage or gearing: how much does it amplify EPS and risk, and how comfortable is it?

Judgement: financial leverage is moderate and comfortable: net debt of about 2.0 times EBITDA by policy (2.3 times at the seasonal H1 peak; 2.6 times including leases on FactSet), interest at about 13 percent of adjusted operating profit, and long-dated fixed-rate notes. Its main role is to fund buybacks that add about 2 percent a year to EPS; its risk is modest rate sensitivity and the "leveraged defensive" label in a risk-off market.

  • Levels: net debt excluding leases $1,058m (FY2024, 1.8 times), $1,330m (FY2025, 2.0 times), $1.53bn (H1 2026, 2.3 times); lease liabilities about $79m; FactSet's lease-inclusive measure peaked at 3.03 times (H1 2023) and is 2.61 times (H1 2026).
  • Cost: weighted average borrowing rate 5.2 percent (FY2025, from 6.0 percent); $500m of 5.3 percent senior notes due 2035 (issued Oct 2025, used to prepay the term loan); revolving credit with 41.9 percent sterling-denominated debt; net interest $71m in FY2025; consensus implies about $75m, $69m and $59m for FY2026 to FY2028.
  • Sensitivity (our arithmetic): with roughly $1bn of floating-rate debt, 100 basis points on rates is about $10m pre-tax, about 2 percent of EPS.
  • Amplification through buybacks: at about 13 times earnings (an earnings yield of about 7.5 percent) and an after-tax cost of debt of about 4 percent, a buyback of 4 to 5 percent of the shares a year adds about 2 percent a year to EPS.
  • Comfort: investment-grade intent; interest cover about 7.5 times adjusted operating profit; peers similar (Coloplast 2.7 times; Siemens Healthineers 3.1 times; Smith & Nephew 1.7 times).

Pricing power and operating leverage (and others)

5a

Pricing power

3 answers
C-1735C-4532P-0438

C-45To what extent does the company have, and use, demonstrable pricing power with minimal erosion of its relative surplus customer utility, shown by evidence of application rather than conjecture?F3230th of 32full answer under 4a Business quality
P-04

After assessing the pricing power in greater detail, how is price likely to contribute to revenue and margin over the next 18 months to four years?

F3820th of 23

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: price will subtract from revenue and margin over the period, not add to them. Like-for-like net price is about +0.3 points of revenue in FY2026, about -0.1 to -0.2 points in FY2027 (France and Germany reforms), about -1.5 points in FY2028 (Medicare competitive bidding, partly offset in revenue by volume that moves to 180 Medical) and about +0.2 points in FY2029 to FY2030 (the Pricing Centre of Excellence and the CPI indexation of Medicare contract prices from their second year). Cumulatively that is about -1.3 points of revenue (about $40m) by FY2030 against a flat-price assumption, and because price drops through almost entirely, about -60 to -90 basis points of adjusted operating margin, concentrated in FY2028. The only positive "price" Convatec has is mix: premium launches add about 1 to 1.5 points a year inside the volume line. Price cannot be used to offset the 2027 input-cost inflation; productivity must.

What the evidence shows on price, by channel (about 85 to 90 percent of revenue is reimbursed or contracted).

Channel (approximate share of group revenue) Price mechanism Like-for-like price over the forecast Evidence
US Medicare direct via 180 Medical (about 7%) and Medicare-dependent distributors (about 2%) Fee schedule, then competitive bidding from 1 Jan 2028 at the 75th percentile of winning bids, CPI-adjusted from the contract's second year Flat to 2027; -20 to -30% retail in 2028 on the 7%; about -15% wholesale on the 2% CMS final rule (Nov 2025); Bernstein models 30% retail and 15% wholesale (4 Jun 2026); DNB Carnegie's base case is about 20% (Oct 2026)
US hospital GPOs and wholesale (ostomy and wound acute) Three-year contracts; rebates About -0.1 to -0.3 points a year GPO concessions cost 27 basis points of gross margin in 2025; Premier AscenDrive commits members to "best-in-market pricing"
Europe, national tariffs and tenders (about 30% of group sales) Tariffs fixed for years then reset; volume caps France and Germany: low-single-digit cuts from Jan 2027 Coloplast (3 Nov 2026); Ottobock: a general 3% German reduction from Jan 2027 (Deutsche Bank, 17 Nov 2026); German law bars wound-care price rises during 2027, then indexation from summer 2028 (Urgo GmbH director, 5 Nov 2026); France's draft 2027 budget proposes a 3.6% tax on reimbursed devices, about €4m for Convatec (Jefferies, 20 Oct 2026)
Infusion sets to pump makers and drug partners (about 19%) Long-term B2B supply agreements Flat; no automatic inflation pass-through Jefferies (13 Apr 2026); terms undisclosed
Rest of the world and private pay Distributors Flat to slightly positive Pricing Centre of Excellence: +30 basis points in FY2025 and H1 2026

How large the price events are for revenue and margin.

  • 2027, Europe. France and Germany are perhaps 8 to 10 percent of group revenue; a 2 to 3 percent cut on that is about -0.2 to -0.3 points of group revenue, partly offset by the Pricing Centre of Excellence (+0.3) elsewhere; net about -0.1 to -0.2 points, about -10 to -15 basis points of margin. If France also enacts the 3.6 percent device tax, add about $4m to $5m of cost (about -15 basis points). This is not in Convatec's guidance commentary and is not visible in consensus segment growth; it is small but certain, and it lands in the same year as the hedge roll-off.
  • 2028, Medicare competitive bidding (the base case: it proceeds). A 20 to 30 percent retail cut on about 7 percent of revenue is a gross price loss of about 1.4 to 2.1 points of revenue, plus about 0.3 points from a 15 percent wholesale cut on the 2 percent sold through Medicare-dependent distributors. Volume moves to the 6 national ostomy and 10 hydrophilic-catheter contract holders, of which 180 Medical (about 40 percent of US catheter home delivery) is the largest, offsetting about 0.5 to 1.0 points. Net revenue effect in the base case: about -1.0 percent (management says 1 to 2 percent; Redburn 1.2 percent). The margin effect is larger than the revenue effect because the cut falls on the retail markup with near-full drop-through: about -75 basis points gross (DNB Carnegie: about 90 basis points for a 30 percent cut), of which about 25 basis points is recovered by own-brand switching (own-brand share at 180 Medical above 60 percent in H1 2026, from about 54 percent in 2022) and cost action. Net about -50 basis points, and about -3.5 percent of FY2028 EPS against a no-bidding path, inside Morgan Stanley's 3 to 8 percent range (13 Oct 2026).
  • The two softeners in the rule that the market under-weights. CMS sets the single payment at the 75th percentile of winning bids, not the lowest, and contract suppliers must furnish the brand the physician prescribes; both protect a branded manufacturer more than prior rounds did. The CPAP and diabetic-testing precedent (retail cuts of about 45 percent) fell almost entirely on distributors, with "negligible direct pricing pressure" on manufacturers (Jefferies, 17 Jul 2025). Convatec is unusual because it is both: the distributor's cut hits it through 180 Medical, and the manufacturer's protection helps it through the brands.
  • The 2028 scenario pair for the Consumer. Deferred (the bipartisan bill of 9 Oct 2026, or CMS delay): FY2028 revenue about +1.0 percent and EPS about +3.5 percent against the base case. Harsher (retail cut 30 percent or more, wholesale above 15 percent, weak volume gains): revenue about -0.8 percent and EPS about -4 to -5 percent against the base case (the bottom of Morgan Stanley's range, -8 percent against a no-bidding consensus). There is a precedent for a delay bill passing: in Jul 2008 Congress delayed the first round of DMEPOS competitive bidding after contracts had been awarded (this is my general knowledge of the Medicare Improvements for Patients and Providers Act, not a finding in the research). I put the chance of deferral at about one in three.

Why price cannot offset 2027 inflation. European hospital contracts do not permit increases for raw-material inflation (former Mölnlycke director, 12 Nov 2026); infusion contracts carry no pass-through; reimbursement resets lag by years; distributors resist manufacturer increases because reimbursement caps their own margin. The 2022 to 2023 precedent confirms the mechanism: Convatec offset 250 basis points of inflation with about 100 basis points of price plus mix and productivity, but that price came largely from exiting low-margin hospital activities, a lever that has been used. So the 2027 inflation line must be met by productivity (P-03), not price.

Mix is the real pricing lever, and it shows up as volume. Soft convex ostomy products carry 20 to 30 percent premiums (Coloplast evidence); hydrophilic catheters are coded separately from Jan 2026 (although the new codes did not raise payment, so the shift is gradual: DNB Carnegie); ConvaNiox lists at £40 a dressing on the UK Drug Tariff; positive mix added 40 basis points of gross margin in H1 2026. The base case carries mix of about +1 to +1.5 points inside the revenue growth of each segment and about +20 to +30 basis points a year of gross margin from 2028.

Against peers. Coloplast guides neutral-to-negative pricing to 2030 and is more exposed to Europe (over half its sales) but much less to Medicare retail (about 6 percent direct); Smith & Nephew and Organogenesis show what a payer can do to a premium category (Organogenesis Advanced Wound Care sales -67 percent over nine months of 2026; Smith & Nephew bioactives -8.8 percent in Q3 2026). Medline's private label at 5 to 15 percent below brands, and private label near 40 percent of US post-acute wound care, cap price at the commodity end. Convatec is therefore a price-taker with one specific, dated price event in 2028 that its peers face less directly.

5b

Operating leverage

2 answers
C-7558P-0156
C-75

How material is the fixed-cost operating leverage: how much does a given change in revenue move operating profit, and how wide a range of outcomes does that create?

B+585th of 32

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: operating leverage is genuine and moderate: roughly 60 percent of revenue is fixed or semi-fixed cost in the short run, contribution margins are about 70 to 75 percent, and realised incremental adjusted operating margins have been 35 to 40 percent because management reinvests part of the leverage. A one-point change in revenue moves adjusted operating profit by about 2 to 3 percent. The range of outcomes is narrow at the annual level but the FY2026 floor is unusually sensitive to overhead discipline in H2.

  • Cost structure (company disclosure, Apr 2026): cost of goods sold about 40 percent of revenue, of which raw materials about 45 percent (about 18 percent of revenue, variable), labour about 25 percent (about 10 percent, semi-fixed), overheads about 30 percent (about 12 percent, fixed); operating expenses about 38 percent of revenue (selling and distribution the largest, R&D about 5 percent, G&A about 6 percent), largely fixed in a year.
  • Realised incremental margins (our arithmetic on company figures): FY2023 to FY2024 about 35 percent ($52m of extra profit on $147m of extra revenue); FY2024 to FY2025 about 39 percent ($59m on $150m). Consensus implies about 38 percent from FY2025 to FY2027 and about 34 percent from FY2027 to FY2028, with operating expenses growing about 3 percent a year against revenue at about 6 to 7 percent.
  • Sensitivities: company and broker estimates put ±1 percent of revenue at about ±1.5 percent of EPS; ±2 percent of volume at about ±3 percent of EPS; ±50 basis points of margin at about ±2.5 percent of EPS.
  • H2 2026 sensitivity: the floor needs H2 operating expenses roughly flat on H1's about $470m at an H2 adjusted gross margin of about 60 percent; in 2025 operating expenses rose about $18m from H1 to H2. Each $10m of H2 overspend costs about 40 basis points of the FY2026 margin, against slack of only about $5m to $10m.
  • Range for FY2027: a margin of 23 to 25 percent on consensus revenue spans adjusted operating profit of about $627m to $682m (about ±4 percent around consensus).
P-01

After assessing the fixed-cost operating leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, against the last four to six years?

B565th of 23

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: operating leverage is real but it will work at about half the pace of the last four years, because the three things that powered 2021 to 2025 (G&A falling from 13 to 6 percent of sales, footprint cuts from nine to five plants, and exits of low-margin lines) are largely harvested, and the next layer of fixed cost (depreciation from $135m to $165m a year of growth capital expenditure, and commercial spend for eight launches against a reinvesting Coloplast) grows with the business. Adjusted operating margin rose about 115 basis points a year from 17.7 percent (2021) to 22.3 percent (2025); the base case has it rising about 55 basis points a year to 25.0 percent by 2030, with a lumpy path: +60 basis points in FY2026 (all in H2), +90 in FY2027, +30 in FY2028 (competitive bidding), then +40 to +50 a year. The incremental adjusted operating margin falls from 35 to 40 percent (FY2023 to FY2025) to about 35 to 38 percent in FY2026 to FY2027 and about 30 percent in FY2028, before recovering to about 32 to 33 percent.

Why the leverage is slower from here: the cost base has changed shape.

  • Of roughly 78 percent of sales that is cost (cost of goods sold about 40 percent; operating expenses about 38 percent), the variable part is raw materials (about 18 percent of sales) and freight and utilities (about 3 percent); labour (about 10 percent) is semi-fixed; manufacturing overhead (about 12 percent), selling and distribution, R&D (about 5 percent) and G&A (about 6 percent) are fixed in the year. Contribution margin is therefore about 75 percent, and a one-point revenue change moves adjusted operating profit by about 3 percent before management response.
  • Realised incremental margins were only 35 to 40 percent because management reinvests half the leverage (R&D from 2.3 to about 5 percent of sales; commercial capability). That behaviour continues: the Accelerate strategy, Natura Body (US launch Q2 2027), GentleCath Air male (US 2027) and ConvaNiox evidence generation all need selling and clinical spend, and Coloplast is putting about 80 percent of DKK 500m of savings into US Chronic Care commercial capability (Deutsche Bank, 6 Nov 2026), which raises the cost of defending and winning US ostomy discharges.
  • A new fixed cost is arriving: depreciation excluding acquired amortisation rises from about $102m (FY2025) to about $122m (FY2027) and about $150m (FY2030) as Infusion and automation lines are validated (Jefferies: "rising slowly towards $150m"). That is about 50 basis points of margin that operating leverage must first cover, which is why the AlphaSense evidence (Redburn, DNB Carnegie) says depreciation "partially dilutes" the automation saving.

The H2 2026 step is the critical near-term test of leverage, and it is mechanical rather than heroic, but it has almost no slack.

  • H1 2026 adjusted operating margin was 21.2 percent ($262m on $1,232m). The floor of at least 23.0 percent on about $2,550m needs about $587m, so H2 needs about $325m on about $1,318m, a margin of about 24.6 percent, a step of about 340 basis points from H1 (H2 2025 was 23.3 percent after 21.3 percent in H1, a step of 200).
  • Management's bridge (Redburn, 5 Aug 2026) is seasonal volume phasing +200 basis points, Infusion mix +50, productivity +80, a smaller InnovaMatrix drag +40: about 370 basis points against the 340 needed, with operating expenses flat across the halves (Bernstein, 26 Jun 2026). The bridge therefore holds about 30 basis points (about $4m to $8m) of cushion. Each $10m of H2 operating-cost overspend or of missed H2 revenue at 75 percent contribution costs about 40 and 30 basis points of the FY2026 margin respectively.
  • The base case is 22.9 percent: the modal outcome is 23.0 to 23.1 percent (the company holds floors with discretionary cost and incentive accruals, as it did through about 40 basis points of currency and 20 of tariffs in 2026), but there is a one-in-three chance of 22.5 to 22.8 percent if Infusion orders or Advanced Wound Care do not accelerate (Deutsche Bank's 6 percent full-year growth forecast needs at least 7 percent in H2).

What leverage does to each line, FY2026 to FY2030 (base case):

Line FY2021 to FY2025 (history) FY2026 FY2027 FY2028 FY2029 FY2030
Revenue growth, reported +7.6, +1.7, +3.4, +6.9, +6.5% +4.6% +6.4% +6.0% +6.8% +6.8%

  • Gross margin carries almost none of the gain until 2028. FY2026 gross margin sits about 60 percent (H1 59.4 percent, with InnovaMatrix taking about 70 basis points); FY2027 is held flat because hedges roll off into polymer and freight inflation (each point of inflation above 3 percent costs $7m to $8m; a doubling of inflation costs $20m to $30m, 75 to 110 basis points), offset by Deeside, Rhymney and the Slovakia, Mexico and Dominican Republic packaging automation (complete by 2027), price of about +30 basis points and Infusion mix. Consensus gross margin of 60.4 percent (FY2027) and 61.3 percent (FY2028) is about 40 to 110 basis points above the base case; the gap is the first set's flagged risk (C-53, C-67) and I agree with it.
  • Operating expenses carry the gain. In the base case operating expenses grow about 3 to 4 percent a year against revenue at 6 to 7 percent: G&A continues to fall as a share (Hyderabad centre from late 2026; agentic AI in finance and quality) while selling and R&D grow with revenue.
  • The width of outcomes from leverage is modest at the annual level. On FY2027 revenue of about $2,712m, a margin range of 23.0 to 24.5 percent spans about $624m to $664m of adjusted operating profit (about ±3 percent around the base case); a ±1 percent revenue miss moves profit about ±2 to 3 percent after partial cost response.

Against peers. Coloplast shows the downside of leverage running in reverse: adjusted EBIT margin fell from about 33 percent (2021) to 24.8 percent in Q4 2025/26 under acquisitions, Hungarian wage inflation and freight, and it now guides a flat mid-20s margin to 2030. Smith & Nephew took years to rebuild margin through cost programmes. Mölnlycke added about 1.7 points of EBITDA margin through its iMpact30+ programme. Convatec is the only one of the four still on a rising margin path, but it is approaching the mid-20s level at which Coloplast chose to stop expanding margin and reinvest; the precedent says the 24 to 26 percent range is where this industry's margins settle, so the base case stops at 25.0 percent in 2030 rather than extrapolating.

5c

Financial leverage

2 answers
C-7660P-0260
C-76How material is the financial leverage or gearing: how much does it amplify earnings per share and risk, and how comfortable is it?D6017th of 32full answer under 4c Financial profile
P-02

After assessing the financial leverage in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share and the balance sheet over the next 18 months to four years?

C+604th of 23

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: financial leverage is a modest, steady, mechanical contributor to EPS and a low risk to the balance sheet. The decisive point for the model is the policy: net debt held at about 2.0 times adjusted EBITDA means net debt rises by $115m to $150m a year as EBITDA grows, and that capacity, plus free cash flow after dividends, is returned through buybacks. In the base case this reduces the average diluted share count from about 1,966m (FY2026) to about 1,686m (FY2030), about 3.8 percent a year, while net interest rises from about $78m to about $99m. Net of the extra interest, leverage-funded buybacks add about 1.5 percent a year to EPS, or about 4 percent cumulatively by FY2029 against consensus's flat share count and falling interest. The balance sheet stays at 1.9 to 2.05 times throughout, with interest cover of 7.5 to 8.3 times adjusted EBIT.

The debt structure, and why the rate risk is small.

  • Net debt excluding leases was $1,330m (FY2025, 2.0 times), $1,530m at H1 2026 (2.3 times, seasonal working capital plus buyback); the base case puts it at about $1,470m at end-2026 (about 2.05 times on the company's EBITDA, which runs about 3 percent above the brokers' basis; the company says "about 2.0 times").
  • $500m of 5.30 percent senior notes run to 2035 with no covenants; the revolving facility was enlarged to $1.0bn and extended to 2031 in Jun 2026, and its covenants were removed; the drawn revolver at end-2025 ($411m) was 41.9 percent sterling, 30.2 percent euro and 27.9 percent dollar. The weighted average borrowing rate fell to 5.2 percent (FY2025) from 6.0 percent. Fitch rates the company BBB, stable (Jun 2025).
  • The company's own sensitivity is $2m of annual interest per 100 basis points (FY2025 composition, after $75m of swaps). I disagree with the first set's "$10m" (C-76). Even with the revolver drawn harder for buybacks the sensitivity is perhaps $4m to $6m, under 1 percent of EPS.
  • The all-in effective cost of net debt (including lease interest and fees) is about 5.3 percent: H1 2026 net finance costs were $38m on average net debt of about $1.43bn. The base case uses 5.3 percent throughout, giving net financial items of about -$78m (FY2026), -$81m, -$87m, -$93m and -$99m (FY2030). Consensus (about -$75m, -$69m, -$59m) is lower because it implicitly deleverages.

How the policy turns into EPS (the key modelling choice).

Base case FY2026 FY2027 FY2028 FY2029 FY2030
Buybacks, USDm -222 (the $200m programme plus $22m in H1) -225 -275 -325 -350
Average diluted shares, m about 1,966 about 1,892 about 1,830 about 1,760 about 1,686

Net debt to company adjusted EBITDA about 2.05x about 2.0x about 2.0x about 1.96x about 1.93x
Net financial items, USDm -78 -81 -87 -93 -99
  • Sizing. I size buybacks at roughly 90 to 100 percent of capacity, defined as free cash flow after dividends plus the increase in debt that keeps leverage at 2.0 times, less about $20m a year of bolt-on acquisitions. FY2027 capacity on that definition is about $240m to $250m: free cash flow about $292m less dividends about $162m plus debt headroom about $115m to $120m, less $20m. It rises to about $360m to $380m by FY2029 to FY2030 as capital expenditure normalises to about 5.5 to 6 percent of sales. Morgan Stanley's "$350m in 2027 and $400m in 2028" (9 Sep 2026) is higher because it starts from a higher cash flow; I think it is too high for FY2027 (it appears to assume the current cash tax rate of about 18 to 20 percent persists and to exclude intangible capital expenditure and leases).
  • Net accretion arithmetic. At about 14 times earnings (earnings yield about 7 percent) and an after-tax cost of debt of about 4 percent, each $100m of debt-funded buyback adds about $3m of net income per year after interest, about 0.7 percent of EPS. Cumulatively the base case's buybacks cut the FY2029 average share count about 6 percent below consensus's implied 1,873m (and about 8 percent below the flat 1,915m consensus carries for FY2028), while adding about $25m to $30m of interest against consensus: net about +4 percent on EPS by FY2029. This is the true size of the "omitted buyback upside"; it is far smaller than the gross "10 percent EPS tailwind" Morgan Stanley quotes, because that figure ignores the interest on the borrowed half.
  • The pence translation is a separate, larger lever on the share price. A move in GBP/USD from 1.34 to 1.39 cuts pence EPS about 3.6 percent on translation alone with nothing changed in dollars (the first set's "over 5 percent" adds the transaction effect of a weaker dollar on sterling and European costs); the buyback buys in pence with dollars, so sterling strength also buys fewer shares.
  • Against consensus's share count. Consensus implies about 1,975m, 1,930m, 1,915m and 1,873m diluted shares for FY2026 to FY2029; the base case is about 1,966m, 1,892m, 1,830m and 1,760m, so the gap widens from about 0.5 percent to about 6 percent by FY2029.

Balance sheet risks, and why they are low but not zero.

  • Interest cover is about 7.5 to 8.3 times adjusted EBIT (the company's covenant measure was 9.4 times in 2025 against a 3.5 times minimum, but covenants have now been removed). There is no refinancing need before 2031.
  • The main balance-sheet risk is a choice, not a constraint: management may exceed 2.0 times for "a compelling bolt-on" (Morgan Stanley, 10 Apr 2026), which would replace buyback accretion with acquisition risk, on a record of $141m of impairments on the largest deal. A second is the new chief financial officer (Jill McConnell, from 1 Mar 2027) resetting capital-return thresholds; nothing suggests she will, but the FY2026 results on 23 Feb 2027 (presented by the outgoing CFO) are the moment a FY2027 buyback is or is not announced.
  • A third is sentiment: in a risk-off regime, the house view is that "the leveraged defensive with a contested estimate de-rates first"; financial leverage at 2.0 times plus a contested H2 margin bridge fits that description until the margin is delivered.

Against peers. Smith & Nephew also runs about 2.1 times but is deleveraging to 1.8 and 1.4 times (Bernstein, 6 Nov 2026), with buybacks tactical; Coloplast is above its 1.5 times target with a payout above its 60 to 80 percent range, so it cannot buy back; Siemens Healthineers borrows at about 2.4 percent against Convatec's 5.2 percent. Convatec is therefore the one peer whose leverage policy compounds EPS mechanically, which is a genuine differentiator but a small one in EPS terms.

5d

Cost-out scope NTM

2 answers
C-7762P-0358
C-77

How much of each major part of the cost base is cuttable or reducible?

A623rd of 32

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement: about 200 to 300 basis points of sales is plausibly reducible over three to four years, concentrated in two places, labour in manufacturing (through automation) and G&A (through shared services and artificial intelligence), which is roughly what the 24 to 26 percent target needs. Selling and R&D are not cuttable without harming the growth target, and raw materials can only be mitigated, not cut.

Cost line (approximate share of sales) Cuttable? Lever and evidence
Raw materials (about 18 percent) Mitigable only Procurement, dual sourcing, design-to-cost; hedging delays rather than removes inflation

P-03

After assessing the scope for cost elimination and efficiency in greater detail, how much is likely to reach the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, and when?

B584th of 23

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: gross efficiency of about 100 to 130 basis points of sales a year is available through FY2028 (the rate the company achieved in H1 2026, "130 basis points of commercial and G&A productivity"), but only about 40 to 60 basis points a year reaches adjusted operating margin, because inflation (the 2027 hedge roll-off), depreciation on the new lines and commercial reinvestment absorb the rest. In dollars: gross efficiency of about $30m to $35m a year, of which about $11m to $17m a year net reaches adjusted operating profit in FY2026 to FY2028 (cumulatively about $40m to $50m by FY2028, about 1.5 points of margin). Together with operating leverage on the fixed cost base, and after the price events of P-04, that produces the base case's climb of about 1.8 points from 22.3 percent (FY2025) to 24.1 percent (FY2028). The net efficiency contribution falls to about $10m a year after 2028 once automation completes, so the further climb to 25.0 percent by 2030 comes mainly from mix and operating leverage. The timing is front-loaded into H2 2026 (flat operating costs) and 2027 (automation completion; Hyderabad), and it reaches operating expenses first and gross margin only from 2028.

Where the remaining cuttable cost is, line by line.

Lever Size of the pool What is left When it reaches the P&L Net of offsets
G&A through Convatec Business Services and AI G&A about 6% of sales (about $150m) Perhaps 50 to 100 basis points more (Hyderabad fourth centre from H2 2026; agentic AI and Copilot in finance, quality and supply chain; Talkdesk gave a 10% agent-efficiency gain at 180 Medical) 2027 to 2028 Mostly retained: about $12m to $25m by 2028
Packaging automation (Slovakia, Mexico, Dominican Republic; Osted and Deeside done; Rhymney automated early 2026) Manufacturing labour about 10% of sales (about $255m); packaging is a part of it A manual line needs ten operators per shift, an automated one two; lines take up to three years to build and validate; programme guided to complete by 2027 Late 2026 to 2028 (FIFO inventory delays gross-margin benefit about six months) About half offset by depreciation; net perhaps $10m to $20m by 2028
Footprint (nine plants to five core sites) Manufacturing overhead about 12% of sales Largely done (EuroTec, Herlev closed; volume to Michalovce) Already in the base Small further gains from utilisation
Procurement, design-to-cost, freight lanes Raw materials about 18% and freight about 2% of sales Mitigation, not elimination Continuous Offsets part of 2027 inflation; no net margin gain
Commercial and pricing centres of excellence Selling the largest opex line Productivity per representative, digital tools Continuous Reinvested in launches and US ostomy defence

Why the gross saving does not all reach the margin.

  • Inflation absorbs about 50 to 110 basis points in FY2027. About 80 percent of FY2026 raw materials are hedged; hedges are 12 months. European polypropylene was up about 45 percent and injection-grade polyethylene about 39 percent year to date in H1 2026, and freight about 40 percent (Morgan Stanley, Jun 2026); the chief executive expects inflation to "double next year" but says it "will not derail" 24 percent. Morgan Stanley (2 Sep 2026) says the company can absorb up to about 6 percent inflation within guidance. Brent below $100 (5 Nov 2026) eases but does not remove this. The base case takes about 70 basis points of gross inflation drag in FY2027.
  • Depreciation absorbs about 40 to 50 basis points by FY2028 (about $102m to about $134m).
  • Reinvestment absorbs the rest. Bernstein (14 Oct 2026) says launch support "may offset near-term productivity gains"; Deutsche Bank expects automation savings to be reinvested rather than push the margin beyond the mid-20s (13 Oct 2026). The company's incentive design (40 percent of the bonus on adjusted operating profit) favours keeping enough to hit the 24 percent mark, not more.

The timing that matters for the 18-month view.

  • H2 2026: operating expenses held flat on H1 (about $470m) is the single largest efficiency contribution in the whole forecast; on Redburn's bridge productivity is +80 basis points of the H2 step. This is fully within management control and is the most reliable element of the H2 bridge.
  • 2027: automation completion and Hyderabad add about 40 to 60 basis points net; this is what offsets the hedge roll-off. Net, the efficiency line and the inflation line roughly cancel at gross margin, and the 2027 margin gain (about +90 basis points in the base case) comes from operating-expense leverage, the absence of InnovaMatrix costs in H1 and Infusion mix.
  • 2028 onward: gross margin begins to rise (automation fully through inventory; mix towards Infusion and premium launches), about +20 to +40 basis points a year, while G&A gains slow.

Peer precedents on how long cost programmes take and how much survives. Coloplast's Funding the Journey (DKK 500m by 2027/28, 75 percent in 2026/27) is about 80 percent reinvested: a reallocation, not a cost-out. Smith & Nephew's 12-Point Plan targets $325m to $375m gross savings by 2027 with factory moves to Malaysia and Memphis, yet margin keeps disappointing on inflation and China pricing. Mölnlycke's iMpact30+ added about 1.7 points of EBITDA margin within about 15 months. The pattern: in this sector gross programmes are delivered, but only a third to a half reaches the margin, and that is the ratio the base case uses for Convatec.

Where I differ slightly from the first set. The first set (C-77, C-78) put the reducible cost at 200 to 300 basis points of sales over three to four years and said that is "roughly what the 24 to 26 percent target needs". I agree on the gross pool, but the target needs only about 170 basis points from FY2025 to reach 24.0 percent, and the evidence says about half the gross pool is consumed by inflation, depreciation and reinvestment. So the pool is sufficient for 24 percent and insufficient on its own for 26 percent; the upper half of the range needs revenue above 7 percent.

5e

Reinforcing feedback loops

2 answers
C-4760P-0555
C-47To what extent do gravitational or mechanical forces, rather than heroic company dynamism, improve the company's competitive advantage and its financial outputs against peers with each period that passes?C6012th of 32full answer under 4a Business quality
P-05

After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, such that dynamics at play today create additional scope for further positive/negative dynamics in the near/mid-term (reinforcing feedback loops), or the opposite (balancing loops), how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

C557th of 23

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

The Investment Analyst's heading After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

Judgement: three reinforcing loops are measurably running now (the Infusion specification-and-capacity loop, the launch loop, and the mechanical leverage-buyback loop), one is real but slow (the hospital-discharge annuity in US ostomy), and one is two-sided (competitive-bidding consolidation at 180 Medical). Against them four balancing loops act on price and cost (payer containment, Coloplast's funded counter-attack in US ostomy, GPO concessions that scale with volume, and the hedge-lag inflation loop). Net, the reinforcing loops lift revenue growth about 1.5 to 2.5 points a year above a 4 to 5 percent market, mostly through Infusion and launches; the balancing loops take back about 0.3 points of price a year on average and about 20 to 40 basis points of margin a year. The timing differs: reinforcing loops dominate to end-2027, the balancing loops dominate 2028, and reinforcing loops modestly dominate again in 2029 to 2030. The discharge loop is too slow to carry the 6 to 8 percent target in 2027; that target rests on Infusion and product launches.

What the loops mean for each line.

  • Revenue: market 4 to 5 percent plus reinforcing loops about 1.5 to 2.5 points less price about 0.3 points gives about 6.0 to 6.8 percent for FY2027 to FY2030 in the base case (FY2028 about 6.0 percent after bidding). Infusion and launches are the load-bearing loops; if the research-leadership gap slows Wave 2 (Natura Body, GentleCath Air male, ConvaFiber, ConvaVac), revenue growth falls towards 5 to 5.5 percent, and that is the most important "loop stall" risk for the third set to test.
  • Margin: the reinforcing loops add mix (Infusion and premium products) and leverage, about +60 to +90 basis points a year gross; the balancing loops take about 20 to 40 basis points a year on average (more in 2027 and 2028), leaving the net +30 to +90 basis points a year in the base case.
  • EPS: the leverage-buyback loop is the only loop wholly within management's control; it supplies about a quarter to a third of EPS growth from FY2027 on.

On the two questions that most need answering.

  • Does US ostomy GPO re-entry convert fast enough to support 6 to 8 percent from 2027? No, not by itself. The installed community base is large and sticky, and the Coloplast precedent shows years of lag. The 2027 target therefore depends on Infusion at about 11 percent, Esteem Body and Natura Body share gains (product-led, not access-led), and Continence launches. The discharge loop is a 2028 to 2030 contributor.
  • Will the payer loop reach ostomy and continence beyond the 2028 round? Within four years, probably only through the 2028 round itself and European tariff resets; ostomy and continence spending grows at about the market rate, not the forty-fold growth that triggered the skin-substitute reset. Beyond 2030 a second bidding round is likely and may cut further. Private payers tend to follow Medicare policy (Bioventus filing, Apr 2026), which is a tail risk to the commercially insured and Medicaid part of 180 Medical's revenue, not a base-case event within four years.

Current guidance and SS management, simple case and no SHMLP risks (and comparable track record)

6a

Guidance conservatism

C+589th of 323 answers
Judgement58C-6855C-7150
Group judgement

Guidance

C+589th of 32

Layer 1 · Guidance

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

Guidance is specific, conservative in style and credible on record, and consensus is anchored to it almost exactly: the FY2026 margin floor, the midpoint of 2027 growth and the bottom of the 2027 margin range. That alignment means the next 18 months are about delivery rather than upgrades: the FY2026 floor is demanding (an H2 step of about 350 basis points with little slack), the 2027 margin target is fair at 24 percent in the face of doubled inflation, and the growth target relies on share gains that the launch pipeline makes plausible. The informal signals add one modelling point: buybacks are management's default and are likely to continue beyond 2026, which consensus does not include. For the model, the Consumer should take FY2026 at the floor with a downside case, FY2027 at 24 percent with inflation explicitly netted, growth at 6 to 7 percent, and a buyback line from 2027 sized to the 2.0 times leverage target.

C-68

To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

C5510th of 32

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: guidance and consensus are aligned, with consensus sitting exactly on the guided FY2026 margin floor, at the midpoint of the 2027 growth range and at the bottom of the 2027 margin range; on EPS consensus is a little above the guided floor. There is no gap that delivery alone would close upwards; upgrades need an H2 2026 beat or capital returns beyond 2026.

  • FY2026: guided adjusted operating margin of at least 23.0 percent against consensus EBIT of $588m on revenue of $2,553m (23.0 percent); guided organic growth excluding InnovaMatrix of 5.5 to 6.5 percent against consensus revenue growth of +4.7 percent reported, which after about 1.6 points of currency and the InnovaMatrix fall (about -2.5 points) is consistent with about 6 percent excluding InnovaMatrix; guided "double-digit" adjusted EPS growth (at least 19.4 cents) against consensus 20.0 cents (+13.6 percent; individual brokers 19.9 to 20.5 cents; Redburn 20.3 cents).

C-71

How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

F5026th of 32

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: near-term guidance is not conservative; mid-term growth guidance is fair to slightly demanding; mid-term margin guidance at its low end is fair. Against the past, the 2027 targets ask for about the same organic growth (6 to 8 percent against 5 to 8 percent over FY2022 to FY2025) and a slower margin climb (about 85 basis points a year against about 115), but in a less favourable external setting (2027 inflation, European price cuts, a reinvesting Coloplast) and with a more favourable internal one (InnovaMatrix gone, a denser launch pipeline, automation, contracted Infusion capacity).

  • FY2026 (not conservative): the floor needs an H2 margin of about 24.7 percent, a sequential step of about 350 basis points from H1 (H2 2025 was 23.2 percent on H1's 21.3 percent, a step of about 190) with only about $5m to $10m of slack; the memo put the odds of meeting it at about 65 percent.
  • 2027 growth (fair to demanding): 6 to 8 percent needs 1.5 to 3.5 points of share gain a year above a 4 to 5 percent market; FY2022 to FY2025 organic averaged about 6 percent (about 6.6 percent excluding InnovaMatrix); the InnovaMatrix base effect helps optically in 2027 only.
  • 2027 margin (fair at 24 percent, demanding at 26): +170 basis points over two years from 22.3 percent, helped by the removal of the InnovaMatrix drag and by automation, hurt by inflation of possibly double the planned 3 percent ($20m to $30m, about 80 to 110 basis points); the past survived a 6.5 to 7 percent inflation spike with margin expansion.
  • Beyond 2027: double-digit EPS needs buybacks; competitive bidding is not explicitly netted in the targets (one research source says the 6 to 8 percent target is "calculated after absorbing future reimbursement changes"; management separately quantifies bidding at 1 to 2 percent of revenue; the Consumer should not assume it is fully absorbed).

Sell-side estimates

C5413th of 32

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The sell-side has modelled the company's own plan: revenue 6 to 7 percent, margin up about 70 to 100 basis points a year to about 24.6 percent by FY2028, EPS growth of 13 to 15 percent, a flat share count after 2026. The plan is credible to FY2027 on the internal change forces and the company's record, which is reliable on growth and precise on margin. The estimate risks are asymmetric in time: FY2026 is set at the floor with little slack, and FY2028 carries an optimistic gross margin and little allowance for competitive bidding, while buybacks after 2026 are an omitted upside of about 2 percent of EPS a year. Estimates have not been cut and guidance has not been rebased, so there is no reset cushion, and the unanimous Buy ratings mean a miss would be cut by every house at once. For the model, the Consumer should regard consensus as the company's guidance translated, stress FY2026 H2 operating costs and FY2027 to FY2028 gross margin, and add a buyback line as a separate, labelled upside.

What else is material or uncommon about the company's guidance or investor relations?

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: five features are uncommon and material for the model: the dollar-reporting and sterling-listing mismatch, an unusually back-end-loaded year, a self-defined cash measure, an accounting roll-off that will change reported earnings sharply in 2027, and a chief financial officer handover timed around the FY2026 results.

  • Currency structure: the only FTSE 100 medtech that reports in dollars; translation drives pence EPS; hedges cover 12 months only.
  • Back-end loading: H1 2026 guided at flat constant-currency margin; H2 at about 25 percent; historically H2 margins exceed H1 by 100 to 190 basis points.

  • Reporting cadence: semi-annual results with four-month and ten-month trading updates; the ten-month update is on 18 Nov 2026.

6c

Case simplicity

7 answers
Judgement64C-7958D-0270D-0352D-0466D-0560D-0666
Group judgement

Risks

–64

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial forecasts for the next 18 months are predictable, and more so after today's update: the earnings cone is narrow (about plus or minus 1.5 percent for FY2026 and minus 5 to plus 4 percent for FY2027), it is now centred on the base case rather than skewed below it, and the base case rests almost entirely on strengths the company has already shown. The earlier answers fit together well; where they jarred, the evidence has settled the point, and in the one case that mattered most (the supposed FY2022 margin miss) it has settled it in the company's favour. I have therefore moved the margin to the guided floor in FY2026 (23.0 percent) and to the bottom of the target range in FY2027 (24.0 percent), which puts the base case level with consensus.

Three qualifications keep this from being a high score. First, the drivers are concentrated: Infusion supplies about 40 percent of growth from a fifth of revenue, the margin rests on one bridge against one external variable, and one plant under an FDA warning letter sits beneath both. Today's acceleration was Infusion and Continence; the two largest segments did not move. Second, the company's disclosure cannot show the contested variable, the margin, until 23 Feb 2027, and cash is already running behind earnings (conversion cut to about 90 percent; leverage ending the year nearer 2.1 than 2.0 times). Third, predictability of earnings is not predictability of the share price: the multiple has risen from about 11.2 to about 14.2 times since September with estimates unchanged, about half of that with the sector, so the price cone is wider and more negatively skewed than the earnings cone.

The steer for the memo: treat the forecasts as reliable to FY2027 and as consensus; treat FY2028 as a base case with one dated binary that resolves in late summer 2027; describe the risk as concentration and price paid, not as doubt about delivery; and expect 2027 to be a year of two halves (I-01).

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

Judgement: the perimeter is narrow by business (four related consumables franchises, five core plants) and wide by geography (about 90 countries, two channel models, a dollar-reporting and sterling-listed structure), and it has been narrowing. Predictability is high on demand and moderate on reported results because of currency, payer events and the semi-annual reporting cadence.

  • Units: Advanced Wound Care 31 percent, Ostomy 28 percent, Continence 22 percent, Infusion 19 percent of FY2025 revenue.

The FY2022 record, corrected. Both earlier sets say the company missed its FY2022 margin floor ("19.5 percent against a floor above 19.7 percent") and use it as the analogue for 2027. The two AlphaSense reports that examine the record, citing the company's Q4 2021 and Q4 2022 calls and its 2022 annual report, say otherwise: FY2022 guidance was a constant-currency margin of at least 18.0 percent, the result was 19.5 percent against consensus of about 19 percent, and the 19.7 percent figure was the opening floor for FY2023, which was raised to at least 20.5 percent in constant currency at the half-year. On that evidence the company has met every margin floor it has set since 2019, through two inflation years. There is one caution, which is my general knowledge and not a finding in the files: my recollection is that currency supplied most of the FY2022 gain and that the constant-currency margin was close to the 18.0 percent floor. If so, the true lesson of the last inflation year is "floor met, with almost no underlying expansion", not "floor missed". That is the right analogue for 2027: at the floor, not above it. I have asked for the record to be verified in the research requests.

What distinguishes the precedents that delivered from those that missed. Smith & Nephew lifted its trading margin by 270 basis points between the halves of 2024 through cost programmes. Medacta issued a profit warning in Nov 2026 when a second-half market recovery it had guided on did not arrive. Coloplast's margins fell when it was integrating acquisitions. The steps that are delivered rest on costs and orders already in hand; the ones that are missed rest on a market turning or on an integration. Convatec's step is of the first kind, and today's update removed the part of it that depended on customers (Infusion orders). Leadership transitions have not prevented comparable delivery elsewhere (Insulet expanded its operating margin by 270 basis points in 2025 while changing both chief executive and chief financial officer).

Where the strengths still to be acquired would bite. Inside 18 months, only in the consensus for Advanced Wound Care (about $739m in FY2026 and $788m in FY2027, against my $724m and $757m) and in any hope that 2027 growth lands in the upper half of the range. The capacity ramp sits in between: the company has automated plants before (Osted doubled; Rhymney automated in early 2026) and today's statement that "improved execution enabled us to meet rising demand ahead of new capacity" is fresh evidence of operating competence at the Infusion plants, but automated lines take up to three years to validate, Ypsomed's comparable projects have taken more than three, and the plant concerned is under an FDA warning letter.

D-03In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?–52full answer under 2a Internal risks
D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

–66

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: narrow by the standards of the sector, now roughly symmetric, and so predictable on earnings: about plus or minus 1.5 percent on FY2026 EPS and about minus 5 to plus 4 percent on FY2027 EPS, with the probability-weighted value on the base case. Today's update removed the largest near-term source of downside (the second-half revenue step) and lifts the chance of meeting the FY2026 margin floor from about two in three to about four in five. Cash is less predictable than earnings, and the cone for the share price is wider and more negatively skewed than either, because the multiple has moved ahead of the estimates.

FY2026 (reported 23 Feb 2027) FY2027 (reported Feb 2028) FY2028 (guided Feb 2028)
Revenue, USDm 2,545 to 2,563 (base 2,554) 2,680 to 2,760 (base 2,717) 2,820 to 2,950 (base 2,880)
Adjusted operating margin 22.7 to 23.3 percent (base 23.0) 23.3 to 24.5 percent (base 24.0) 23.5 to 25.0 percent (base 24.3)
Adjusted EPS, cents 19.8 to 20.4 (base 20.1) 21.8 to 23.8 (base 23.0) 23.4 to 27.4 (base 25.6)
Width About plus or minus 1.5 percent Minus 5 to plus 4 percent Minus 9 to plus 7 percent

Scenarios (my probabilities; the second set had bear 25 percent and bull 20 percent):

  • Bear, about 20 percent: November and December slip or currency worsens and FY2026 lands at about 22.7 percent; in 2027 Infusion slows against its comparatives, wound care and Europe stay soft, inflation runs above 6 percent and growth is about 5.5 percent at a margin of about 23.3 percent; bidding proceeds with a 30 percent cut. EPS about 19.8, 21.8 and 23.4 cents.
  • Base, about 55 percent: 20.1, 23.0 and 25.6 cents.
  • Bull, about 25 percent: FY2026 margin of about 23.2 percent; 2027 growth of about 7.3 percent at about 24.4 percent with a buyback of $250m or more; bidding deferred. EPS about 20.3, 23.8 and 27.4 cents.
  • The probability-weighted FY2027 EPS is 23.0 cents, the base case. The second set's cone was skewed down (base 1 percent below consensus, bear 7 percent below, bull 4 percent above); the skew has gone because the revenue step is delivered and the margin lean is removed.

What sets the width, in order of weight on EPS:

  1. 2027 input costs against productivity. Each point of inflation above the planned 3 percent costs $7m to $8m, about 1.3 percent of EPS; a doubling costs $20m to $30m, 3.5 to 5 percent of EPS before mitigation. The evidence is mixed: polymer contract prices are up 35 to 40 percent, but polyethylene plants are running at a record-low 73 percent of capacity, which limits further rises; ocean freight should ease as the fleet grows 9.5 percent while US trucking rates rise about 5 percent; packaging stays dear into 2027. Coloplast expects 3 to 4 percent and missed its last quarter's margin by 210 basis points on it. The 2027 hedge cover is not disclosed until February.
  2. The FY2026 margin itself. Each $10m of second-half cost or lost contribution is about 40 basis points and about 2 percent of EPS. I put the chance of 23.0 percent or better at about four in five: the floor was restated with ten months of the year known and after absorbing a further 25 basis points of currency.
  3. Infusion. After about 20 percent in July to October the second-half 2027 comparatives are demanding, and new capacity must be validated during 2027. Each point of Infusion growth is about 0.2 points of group growth and about 0.6 percent of EPS.
  4. Currency. A 10 percent move in the dollar is about 4 percent of EPS. Sterling has slipped to about 1.32 dollars from the 1.34 the earlier sets used, which adds about 1.5 percent to pence EPS. Today's update shows the two currency effects do not move together: the revenue benefit fell and the margin headwind rose.
  5. Europe. The French levy, as drafted, would cost Convatec about €10.8m, 2.1 percent of pre-tax profit (Jefferies), about four times the average for the companies Jefferies covers; the draft budget was rejected in parliament and the final law is due in late December; I put about one in four on a levy in some form. Germany cuts reimbursement by 3 percent from Jan 2027 (Ottobock) and is tightening the evidence required for wound dressings (an URGO manager expects German wound-care sales to fall 5 to 10 percent in 2027). Convatec does not disclose its French or German revenue.
  6. Buyback size and timing: about plus or minus 1 percent of FY2027 EPS.
  7. Competitive bidding (FY2028): plus or minus 3.5 to 4.5 percent, and most of this is settled inside the 18 months, when CMS publishes the payment amounts in late summer 2027. I keep the second set's odds of a deferral at about three in ten. The 2008 precedent has a sting the earlier sets did not mention: Congress paid for that delay with a 9.5 percent cut to the fee schedule, so a deferral is relief, not an all-clear.

Tails outside the cone (each low probability). An escalation of the FDA action at Reynosa: the letter concerns leak-test validation and adverse-event reporting, MiniMed's filings link undetected leaks to patient-injury complaints, and Bernstein notes that an inadequate response could lead to an injunction or consent decree (Becton Dickinson's infusion-pump business has been under one since 2007). A new CMS action on chronic care. A recall. A pump partner taking sets in-house (Tandem bought a set developer in 2022, and its tubeless pump contains no Convatec components).

Cash is less predictable than earnings. Equity cash conversion was cut today to about 90 percent. FY2026 free cash flow on the full definition is about $190m to $220m (the second set had $233m); meeting even that needs a second-half working-capital release of about $180m, near the top of the range of the last five years ($91m to $192m). Net debt should end 2026 at about $1.5bn, about 2.1 times EBITDA, not 2.0 times.

The share price cone. On unchanged forward estimates, a fall to a trough of 10.5 to 11.0 times is about 198p to 207p (minus 23 to minus 26 percent from 268p), and with a 5 to 8 percent earnings miss as well about 182p to 188p (minus 30 to minus 32 percent); the five-year median of 16.55 times is about 312p (plus 16 percent). Earnings growth improves this over time (by the end of 2027: about plus 18 percent with no change in the multiple, plus 36 percent at the median, minus 15 percent at a trough after a miss), but at today's price the first move in either direction is larger on the downside.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

–60

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: helpful on growth and unusually specific in its building blocks, but blind on the variable the case turns on. Between early August and late February the company publishes no profit figure, so the next six months contain exactly one margin observation (23 Feb 2027), and the trading updates in between give segment growth as bands, not numbers. The case can be monitored well for demand and guidance wording, and only indirectly for margin, cash and the 2027 cost base.

What the company gives (today's update is a fair example). Organic growth with and without InnovaMatrix; segment direction; guidance block by block, including the effect of currency on revenue and on margin in basis points, the InnovaMatrix margin headwind, capital expenditure, finance cost, book and cash tax rates, the cash cost of adjusting items and cash conversion; a share count after every day's buyback; dated product and capacity milestones; sensitivities for inflation and currency.

What it does not give.

  • Any margin or profit figure between half-year and full-year results.
  • Segment growth as numbers in the trading updates ("double-digit" for Infusion covers anything from 10 percent upward; I have had to back-solve about 20 percent for July to October).
  • Segment profit, so the Infusion mix effect in the margin bridge cannot be audited.
  • Gross margin guidance, and the hedge cover for 2027 (it gave 80 percent for 2026).
  • Revenue in France and Germany, where prices are being cut.
  • Patients gained through the Vizient and Premier contracts.
  • Infusion capacity in units, and the validation milestones of the new line.
  • A consistent cash measure (redefined in 2025; its guidance cut today).
  • The second-half margin bridge in its own documents (it reaches us through brokers).

The six months to mid-May 2027, and what each date can tell the Consumer:

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

–66

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement: the internal chain is clear and can be followed line by line from target to EPS, with two breaks (no segment profit, no gross margin guidance). The external chain is clear for the payer and for inflation, where the effects are dated and quantified, and unclear for currency and for European pricing. Today's update is the illustration: growth guidance rose, margin and EPS guidance did not, and the reason was currency.

The internal chain, link by link.

  1. Target to driver. 6 to 8 percent growth is broken into segment targets (Infusion double digit; Ostomy and Continence mid-to-high single digit from 2027; Advanced Wound Care high single digit from 2028) and into named products and contracts with dates.
  2. Driver to revenue line. Four segments are reported, with InnovaMatrix separated, so each driver can be checked against a line.
  3. Revenue to cost and margin. The company quantifies its bridges (H1 2026: InnovaMatrix minus 140 basis points, productivity plus 130, currency minus 60; the second-half step: seasonal volume 200, Infusion mix 50, productivity 80, InnovaMatrix 40) and gives general and administrative costs as a share of sales.
  4. Margin to EPS. Finance cost and tax rate are guided; the share count is published daily during a buyback; dividends follow a stated payout. The rules of thumb hold together: one point of organic growth is about $15m of profit, 2.5 to 3 percent of EPS, before the reinvestment the company normally makes (about half that after it); 50 basis points of margin is about $13m and 2.5 percent of EPS.
  5. In the model. The same chain works mechanically: EBITDA less net financial items, book tax, capital expenditure, leases and the change in working capital reproduces the model's FY2026 free cash flow exactly. The chain breaks only at the share count, which is a fault, not an ambiguity.

The two internal breaks. Without segment profit, the Infusion mix effect and the cost of the wound-care launches cannot be observed. Without gross margin guidance, the split of the 2027 margin between gross margin (where inflation lands) and operating costs (where management has control) has to be inferred.

The external chain.

Dynamic Path to EPS How clear
US Medicare bidding A cut of 20 to 30 percent to retail prices on the 7 percent of revenue billed by 180 Medical, with 70 to 90 percent falling to profit: 1 to 2 percent of revenue, 3 to 8 percent of FY2028 EPS; offset by volume from the consolidation of suppliers to six national ostomy and five urological contracts Clear and dated, though quantified mainly by brokers. Second-order effects are not: private insurers tend to index their prices to Medicare's, and winning distributors press manufacturers
Input costs Raw materials are about 45 percent of cost of goods sold; hedges and five months of stock delay price moves by six to twelve months; $7m to $8m per point of inflation in 2027; no pass-through in Infusion contracts Clear and quantified by the company
Currency Translation of revenue, a transactional effect on costs in Mexico, Slovakia, Denmark and the UK, 12-month hedges, and a share price in sterling Unclear: today the revenue benefit fell and the margin headwind rose at once
European pricing France (a proposed levy on reimbursed devices) and Germany (a 3 percent cut and evidence rules for wound dressings) Unclear: no exposure disclosed, timing disputed
Partner demand Pump and drug partners' sales drive set volumes Observable through partners' results; contract prices and margins undisclosed

A pattern worth the Consumer's attention. Relative to its size, Convatec is more exposed than Coloplast to both of the next two payer measures: 7 percent of revenue against about 1 percent billed directly to Medicare, and 2.1 percent of pre-tax profit against 0.9 percent for the French levy (Jefferies). In the United States the reason is clear: the cut lands on the retail operator, and Convatec owns it. In France the research does not give the reason; Convatec also owns home-delivery businesses there, which may be why, but that is my conjecture. Either way, the vertical integration that gives Convatec the patient and the second margin also puts more of its profit where payers act first.

C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

C+628th of 32

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement: the company is a conservative setter that raises organic-growth guidance when the year allows and meets margin guidance rather than beating it; it de-risks bad news early (InnovaMatrix guided down twice ahead of the numbers). This is the "sandbagging precision" archetype for growth and the "honest tracker" archetype for margin. The new chief executive has kept the style but has not yet raised a near-term number.

  • Raises: FY2024 organic guidance raised at the half to 7.25 to 8.0 percent (delivered 7.7 percent); FY2025 narrowed to the top half, 6.0 to 6.5 percent (delivered 6.4 percent excluding InnovaMatrix); mid-term growth raised to 6 to 8 percent (Feb 2026).
  • Holds and narrows: FY2026 margin floor held through a larger currency headwind; FY2026 growth range narrowed symmetrically (Aug 2026).
  • Cuts: InnovaMatrix to about $20m (Feb 2026), then to $5m to $10m (Aug 2026), each ahead of the decline.
  • Margin: met (FY2023 to FY2025), missed once by about 20 basis points (FY2022, inflation); never beaten materially.
  • Behaviour under Mason: "consistently under-promises and over-delivers" (leadership research); guidance set with buffers to absorb shocks (the floor absorbed about 40 basis points of currency and about 20 of tariffs).
C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

C+6310th of 32

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement: over five years Convatec has met or beaten its organic-growth guidance every year and met its margin guidance in all but one (FY2022, in an inflation spike, the environment most comparable to the 2027 risk); against consensus it typically meets revenue and margin and beats EPS through below-the-line items. The last twelve months were mixed at the group level only because of InnovaMatrix.

  • Last year: FY2025 revenue $2,439m against $2,429m consensus (+0.4 percent); organic 4.8 percent against 5.0 percent consensus (slight miss, InnovaMatrix); margin 22.3 percent in line; EPS 17.6 cents against 16.8 cents (a beat from lower finance costs and a stable tax rate). Four-month 2026 update slightly below consensus. H1 2026: revenue in line ($1,232m); group organic 1.8 percent against 2.2 percent consensus (miss) and 5.0 percent excluding InnovaMatrix in line; margin 21.2 percent against 20.9 percent (beat); adjusted operating profit $262m against $258m; EPS 8.5 cents against 8.4 cents.
  • Three years: FY2023 beat on organic growth (7.2 percent); FY2024 beat a raised range (7.7 percent) and met margin (21.2 percent); FY2025 hit.
  • Five years: FY2022 organic 5.6 percent within 4.5 to 6.0 percent; margin 19.5 percent against a floor above 19.7 percent, a miss on cost inflation of 6 to 7 percent, the comparable condition for 2027.
  • Pattern: reliability on growth, precision on margin, below-the-line help on EPS.
P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

C558th of 23

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement: the record justifies high weight on management's revenue guidance, moderately high weight on its margin floors (met precisely, never beaten, missed once in an inflation year), and low weight on anything beyond 2027, where there is no guidance. The forecasts should be leaned slightly down on margin in FY2026 and FY2027 (about 10 to 20 basis points below the floor and the low end), slightly down on revenue in FY2027 and FY2028 (6.5 percent ex-InnovaMatrix organic in FY2027 against a 7 percent midpoint; bidding in 2028), and up on the share count from FY2027 (buybacks under the stated policy). Net, EPS is leaned 1 to 3 percent below consensus for FY2026 to FY2028 and roughly level with it by FY2029 to FY2030, while free cash flow is leaned below consensus on the cash tax rate and full capital expenditure.

The record, read for what it predicts.

Measure FY2022 FY2023 FY2024 FY2025 FY2026 so far What it says for the forecast
Organic growth against initial guidance 5.6% in 4.5 to 6.0% 7.2%, a beat 7.7% in a range raised at the half to 7.25 to 8.0% 6.4% ex-InnovaMatrix, top half of 6.0 to 6.5% 5.0% ex-InnovaMatrix in H1; range narrowed symmetrically to 5.5 to 6.5% Reliable to the range; raises come at the half, not at the start
Adjusted operating margin against guidance 19.5% against a floor above 19.7%, a miss in a 6 to 7% inflation year 20.2%, met 21.2%, met 22.3%, met 21.2% in H1, above consensus 20.9%; floor at least 23.0% held Precise; never beaten materially; the one miss was in the condition most like 2027
EPS against consensus 17.6c against 16.8c, a beat from finance costs and tax 8.5c against 8.4c (H1) Below-the-line items tend to help
Group revenue against consensus +0.4% H1 in line; four-month update slightly below In line

How much weight, by year.

  • FY2026 (seven weeks left): high weight. The year is 80 percent reported or in the order book; the uncertainty is the H2 margin step. EPS cone about 19.4 to 20.2 cents; the base case 19.8 cents.
  • FY2027: moderately high weight on revenue, moderate on margin. Growth targets have been met in four of four years; the margin target faces the inflation roll-off (the FY2022 analogue), a new chief financial officer from 1 Mar 2027, a Global Operations leader still being recruited during the final automation year, and Coloplast's reinvestment. EPS cone about 21.6 to 23.8 cents; base case 22.7 cents.
  • FY2028: moderate weight. One binary (bidding) is worth about ±3.5 percent of EPS, and there is no guidance beyond 2027; the company will set a new mid-term framework with the FY2027 results (Feb 2028). EPS cone about 23.5 to 27.0 cents; base case 25.2 cents.
  • FY2029 to FY2030: low weight. Extrapolation of the structural rates (revenue 6.5 to 7 percent; margin +40 to +50 basis points a year; buybacks at policy). Only three to four brokers forecast FY2029.

Which direction to lean, and why.

  • Margin down, slightly, in FY2026 and FY2027. The archetype is "honest tracker" on margin: the modal outcome is the floor, and the tail is to the downside because the company adds leverage back into investment when it can. In FY2026 the bridge has about 30 basis points of cushion (P-01). In FY2027 the 24 percent figure is what management has named informally; Redburn calls the upper end dependent on "flawless execution". The base case therefore takes 22.9 percent and 23.8 percent, against the floor of 23.0 percent and the low end of 24.0 percent.
  • Revenue down, slightly, in FY2027 and FY2028. The growth archetype is "sandbagging precision", but the FY2027 target needs a step that the company has not yet shown on the underlying (ex-InnovaMatrix) rate: 5.0 percent in H1 2026, about 6.4 percent needed in H2 2026, and 6.5 to 7 percent for 2027. Advanced Wound Care ex-InnovaMatrix grew 3.4 percent in H1 2026 and consensus needs a marked acceleration; Europe cuts price from Jan 2027; Coloplast reinvests. The base case is 6.5 percent ex-InnovaMatrix organic in FY2027 (reported +6.4 percent at flat currency) against consensus +6.8 percent, and FY2028 includes bidding.
  • Share count up (more buybacks), from FY2027. The record of acting on capital returns is strong ($300m executed within five months in 2025; $200m in 2026) and the policy is explicit; consensus carries a flat share count and falling interest.
  • Do not lean the InnovaMatrix base effect upward. As set out in the reading notes, FY2027 reported growth gets almost nothing from InnovaMatrix; only the optics of group organic growth change.

What would change the lean. A ten-month update (18 Nov 2026) at or above 6 percent ex-InnovaMatrix year to date with Infusion at double digits, plus an FY2026 margin of 23.2 percent or more on 23 Feb 2027, would remove the margin lean and would justify FY2027 at 24.0 to 24.2 percent; that would be the company's first margin beat in five years and the "beat-and-raise" the sector history says the shares need. Conversely, a ten-month figure below 5.5 percent year to date would make 22.5 percent the FY2026 base and put the FY2027 margin at about 23.3 percent.

Growth rate

Group judgement

Financial forecasts

B555th of 23

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The financial forecasts are steady, moderately positive and slightly below consensus in the near term, with a quality of earnings that is improving. Convatec should grow revenue about 6 to 7 percent a year (faster than its six-year record), lift its adjusted operating margin from 22.3 to about 25.0 percent by 2030 (half its past pace, reaching 24 percent a year after the target), and compound adjusted EPS at about 13 percent a year, with buybacks under a 2.0 times leverage policy supplying a quarter to a third of that growth and free cash flow roughly doubling as capital expenditure normalises. The forecasts are leaned slightly below the company's named margin floors and below the guidance midpoint on FY2027 growth, because the one comparable precedent (FY2022, an inflation year) is the company's one margin miss and because 2027 brings the hedge roll-off, European price cuts, Coloplast's reinvestment and a new chief financial officer at once; they carry competitive bidding in FY2028 at the low end of management's range. The consequence for the third and fourth sets: consensus is the company's plan translated and sits about 1 to 3 percent above the base case on EPS for FY2026 to FY2028, while being internally inconsistent with the stated leverage policy (flat share count with falling interest). The case is predictable on demand and narrow on outcomes, but its two concentrated drivers (the H2 2026 to 2027 margin bridge and Infusion volume) and one binary (bidding) are where predictability will be won or lost.
