What would each outcome (raise, meet, cut) at the FY2026 results need to happen? Focus: Whether FY2026 guidance is more likely to be met at the floor, beaten or missed, what FY2027 guidance the company is likely to give on 23 Feb 2027, and whether consensus for FY2027 and FY2028 is more exposed to upgrades from buybacks or to cuts from margin, European pricing and competitive bidding.

Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 18 November 2026, 11:45 to 12:15 UK, Fable, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Convatec (CTEC-GB): third and fourth sets of questions, the predictability of the forecasts and what guidance, consensus and the narrative already reflect

As at 18 Nov 2026. Written for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Reading notes for the Consumer (read before the answers)

1. The ten-month trading update was published this morning, after every file I was given was written. The AlphaSense reports all say it had not yet happened. I read the company's announcement and the first press reports (sources at the end of these notes). It said:

  • Growth. Organic revenue growth excluding InnovaMatrix of 6.6 percent for the ten months to 31 Oct 2026 (5.0 percent in H1); 3.7 percent including InnovaMatrix (1.8 percent in H1); reported growth 4.8 percent, including a currency translation benefit of about 1.1 points.
  • Segments, ten months. Infusion Care "accelerated to double-digit" (7.4 percent in H1), on faster diabetes growth in the second half and AbbVie's Parkinson's therapy; Continence Care "accelerated to mid-to-high single digit" (5.9 percent in H1), on US new-patient volumes and growth outside the US; Ostomy Care mid single digit, "similar to H1" (4.3 percent); Advanced Wound Care excluding InnovaMatrix low-to-mid single digit, "similar to H1" (3.4 percent).
  • FY2026 guidance. Organic growth excluding InnovaMatrix raised to 6.25 to 7.0 percent (from 5.5 to 6.5 percent); Infusion raised to double digit (from high single digit) and Continence to mid-to-high single digit (from mid single digit); InnovaMatrix revenue about $5m (from $5m to $10m). Adjusted operating margin "at least 23 percent" unchanged, now absorbing a currency headwind of about 65 basis points (previously about 40) and an InnovaMatrix headwind of about 90 basis points. Double-digit adjusted EPS growth unchanged. The currency benefit to revenue cut to about 90 basis points (from 130). Capital expenditure at the upper end of $200m to $230m. Adjusted net finance expense about $75m. Adjusted tax rate about 23 percent; cash tax rate about 16 percent. Cash cost of adjusting items about $30m (from $20m). Equity cash conversion about 90 percent (from about 100 percent). The $200m buyback on track to complete this year.
  • Medium term. Targets reiterated: 6 to 8 percent organic growth, a mid-20s adjusted operating margin from 2027, double-digit EPS growth. 2027 milestones restated: new Infusion capacity, Natura Body in mid-2027, ConvaNiox diabetic-foot-ulcer trial results, full US launches of ConvaVac and ConvaFiber.
  • Share price. Up about 9 percent to about 268p (intraday high about 273p), the best performer in the FTSE 100, from an implied close of about 247p on 17 Nov; a new 52-week high. Every price-based figure in the first two sets (written at 252.8p) is now out of date. At 268p the FactSet 24-month-forward P/E is about 14.2 times (13.37 times on 13 Nov), which is the two-year median (14.31 times) and still below the five-year 25th percentile (14.60 times).

2. What the update means, on my arithmetic (the company gave ten-month figures only).

  • Growth excluding InnovaMatrix was about 9 percent in the four months July to October. Infusion grew about 20 percent in those four months and supplied about half of the four months' growth from a fifth of revenue; Infusion and Continence together (41 percent of revenue) supplied about three-quarters. Ostomy and Advanced Wound Care (59 percent of revenue) did not accelerate.
  • The new FY2026 range implies 4.6 to 8.9 percent for November and December, and about 6.7 percent at the midpoint. If the July-to-October rate simply continued, the year would finish at the top of the range.
  • It is a raise of organic growth, not of dollars or profit. About $15m of extra organic revenue is offset by about $10m less currency benefit and $2.5m less InnovaMatrix. FY2026 revenue is about $2,545m to $2,563m against consensus $2,553m; adjusted operating profit at the floor is about $587m against consensus $588m; EPS is about 20.1 cents against consensus 20.0 cents. Consensus organic growth should rise; consensus EPS should hardly move. The 9 percent share-price rise is therefore a rise in the multiple on a cleared worry, not the start of an earnings upgrade cycle.

By half-year: H2 2026 revenue about $1,322m (+5.0 percent) at an adjusted operating margin of about 24.6 percent; H1 2027 about $1,323m (+7.4 percent) at about 23.0 percent; H2 2027 about $1,394m (+5.5 percent) at about 25.0 percent. My base case is now level with consensus for FY2026 and FY2027 and about 2 percent below it for FY2028 (competitive bidding).

4. Where I disagree with the first and second sets (each is explained in the answer named):

  1. The FY2022 margin "miss" is not supported by the evidence here (D-02). Both sets lean the margin down because of "one miss, in the inflation year most like 2027". Two AlphaSense reports, citing the company's own calls, say the FY2022 guidance was a constant-currency margin of at least 18.0 percent and the result was 19.5 percent; the "19.7 percent" floor was the initial FY2023 guidance.
  2. The central margin case should be "delivered at the floor", not 10 to 20 basis points below it (D-02, D-04). I move FY2026 to 23.0 percent and FY2027 to 24.0 percent.
  3. The next buyback is more likely to be announced with the interim results in August 2027 than on 23 Feb 2027 (I-01). Both programmes so far were launched in August; leverage will end 2026 at about 2.1 times, not 2.0 times.
  4. FY2026 free cash flow is about $205m, not $233m, and the French device levy, if enacted as drafted, is about €10.8m (2.1 percent of pre-tax profit), not about €4m (D-04).
  5. The FDA warning letter is more serious in kind than "complaint handling" (D-03, D-04). It cites failures to validate the leak test for infusion sets and to report adverse events; a customer's own filings link undetected leaks to patient-injury complaints. There is still no restriction on production.
  6. 2027 will be a year of two unequal halves (I-01, I-02). H1 2027 laps a depressed H1 2026; H2 2027 laps the surge just reported. Neither earlier set, and no broker I have seen, frames it this way.

5. What I could not see. The transcript of today's call (behind a paywall), so management's remarks on 2027 inflation, hedging and buybacks are not captured; any broker reaction to the update; any direct investor commentary on social media (the AlphaSense narrative report holds sell-side and expert material only); Coloplast's price history in the co-movement table (the closest peer is missing from it).

Sources beyond the files (all 18 Nov 2026 unless dated): Convatec, "Trading update for the ten months ended 31 October 2026" (https://www.marketscreener.com/news/convatec-trading-update-for-the-ten-months-ended-31-october-2026-ce785edcd18ffe20); Alliance News, "Convatec shares rise as increases sales outlook as growth accelerates" (https://www.marketscreener.com/news/convatec-shares-rise-as-increases-sales-outlook-as-growth-accelerates-ce785eddd988f421); Proactive, "Convatec shares surge 10% as medical products group upgrades revenue outlook" (https://uk.finance.yahoo.com/news/convatec-shares-surge-10-medical-084500253.html); Investing.com, "ConvaTec raises 2026 revenue guidance on stronger growth" (https://www.investing.com/news/stock-market-news/convatec-raises-2026-revenue-guidance-on-stronger-growth-93CH-5034964); Proactive, 21 Oct 2026, "JP Morgan says ConvaTec guidance looks increasingly secure ahead of trading update" (https://uk.finance.yahoo.com/news/jp-morgan-says-convatec-guidance-133000039.html).

The Financial Analyst's model: the flagged gaps explained

The earnings-per-share gap (the Analyst's 0.20, 0.23, 0.25, 0.29, 0.33 against the model's -3.70, -4.41, -2.73, -1.97, -1.56) is a fault in the model's share count, not in any forecast input. The model's net income matches the second set's in every year (389.6, 429.5, 461.8, 504.9 and 549.1 against 390, 429, 461, 503 and 548), so everything down to net income is right. The fault is below it:

  • Diagnosis. The model's forecast share count appears to start from zero instead of from the shares actually in issue (I cannot see the workbook; this is inferred from the summary). If the opening balance is zero and each year adds the 6m shares from share plans and subtracts the buyback at about $3.45 a share (the 16 Nov price of 2.56 GBP in dollars), the share count is about minus 55m at the end of 2026, minus 114m at the end of 2027, minus 188m, minus 276m and minus 372m. Dividing each half-year's net income by those figures reproduces the model's EPS for all five years (I get -4.36, -2.74, -1.99 and -1.58 for FY2027 to FY2030 against the model's -4.41, -2.73, -1.97 and -1.56). The likeliest cause is an empty share-count cell for the last reported period (30 Jun 2026), like the empty lease-payment cells the summary already reports.
  • What else the fault corrupts. (i) Dividends paid: the model multiplies the dividend per share by a negative share count, so dividends become a small cash inflow instead of an outflow of about $150m to $210m a year. That is why the model's net debt falls from 1,468 to 1,028 between 2026 and 2030, when the policy and the second set's intention have it rising with EBITDA. (ii) The return at the guideline fair multiple (minus 1,221.7 percent and minus 954.2 percent; a value per share of minus 28.67 GBP) and the "two-year-forward P/E today of minus 1.0 times" are meaningless. They must not go into the memo.
  • What is unaffected. Revenue, EBITDA, EBITA, pre-tax profit, net income and equity free cash flow. Net financial items are a direct input, so the wrong net debt does not feed back into earnings.
  • Three further points for the repair. (i) FactSet's share count (2,049.4m at the end of 2025) appears to include treasury shares: the company reported 1,911.1m shares in issue excluding 138.7m in treasury in its buyback announcements of mid-November 2026, which sum to 2,049.8m. The right opening figure is the count excluding treasury shares, about 1,955m at the end of 2025 and 1,911m now; starting from 2,049m would understate EPS by about 5 to 7 percent. (ii) The model appears to buy back at a flat price of about $3.45 in every year, whereas the second set assumed $3.50 rising to $4.40; at a flat price the model retires about 45m more shares by 2030, which would overstate FY2030 EPS by about 2 percent against the second set's cross-check. (iii) The model's net debt of 1,467.7 at the end of 2026 looks like the second set's "about 1,470" but is not the same thing: the model's figure is FactSet's, which includes about $115m of leases, and the second set's excludes them.

The Financial Analyst should set the opening share count of the forecast to the shares in issue excluding treasury shares (about 1,955m at 31 Dec 2025; 1,911.1m in mid-November 2026, from the company's "Transaction in Own Shares" announcements), check that the last reported period (30 Jun 2026) holds a share count, confirm which share price the model uses for buybacks in each year, and then re-run EPS, dividends paid, net debt and the return at the guideline multiple. The Financial Analyst should also restate the H1 2026 actual EBITDA to the adjusted basis (about $316m, 25.6 percent of revenue: adjusted operating profit of $262m plus about $54m of depreciation and amortisation excluding acquired intangibles) in place of FactSet's operating EBITDA of $291m, so that the H2 2026 column does not need to be "moved by 1.7 points" to an EBITDA margin of about 30.5 percent that nobody forecasts.

The free-cash-flow gap (the Analyst's 233, 292, 362, 412, 451 against the model's 239.1, 307.0, 382.7, 428.5, 467.6) is definitional, 3 to 6 percent, and shows no wrong input. The model's figure is EBITDA less net financial items, tax at the book rate, capital expenditure, lease payments and the change in working capital; that formula reproduces 239.1 exactly for FY2026. The second set's cross-check also deducts the cash cost of adjusting items ($20m to $30m a year, for which the input sheet has no line) and uses a cash tax rate below the book rate in FY2026 and FY2027. Today's update does change the FY2026 cash inputs (capital expenditure at the top of the range; equity cash conversion cut to about 90 percent), and I have changed those.

The basis of the model's history. The FY2025 "actual" column is FactSet's (EBITDA 630, EBITA 406, net income 243, EPS 0.12), while the forecasts are on the company's adjusted basis (FY2025: EBITDA 646, adjusted operating profit 544, net income about 360, EPS 0.176). Growth from FY2025 to FY2026 read off the model's lines mixes the two (EBITA up 44 percent). True growth on the adjusted basis is about 8 percent for adjusted operating profit and 14 percent for EPS.

What the return at the guideline multiple will show once repaired (my hand arithmetic, to be replaced by the model's). On my EPS path the 24-month-forward EPS rises about 15 percent between now and 31 Dec 2027 (from about 25.2 cents to FY2029's 29.0 cents). From 268p, with dividends of about 2.4 percent: no change in the multiple gives about +18 percent; the five-year median of 16.55 times gives about +36 percent; the guideline 19.27 times gives about +58 percent (about +66 percent from the 2.56 GBP the model uses); a fall to an 11 times trough with EPS 7 percent lower gives about -15 percent. More than half of the guideline return is the multiple rising about 35 percent in 13 months. 19.27 times is above the five-year 90th percentile (18.88 times), between the ten-year 75th and 90th percentiles (18.7 and 20.8 times), and at or above where Coloplast trades today (about 18 times). I regard 14.5 to 16.5 times as the defensible range for the end of 2027 (D-07).

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of CTEC-GB with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-11-17 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Convatec Group plc (CTEC-GB), a London-listed, US-dollar-reporting maker of chronic-care medical consumables in four segments: Advanced Wound Care (Aquacel, ConvaFoam, ConvaNiox, ConvaVac), Ostomy Care (Esteem Body, Natura Body), Continence Care (GentleCath catheters and the US home-delivery distributor 180 Medical) and Infusion Care (infusion sets for insulin pumps and subcutaneous Parkinson's therapies).

Request: OWN-1

Question: What did the management of Convatec (CTEC-GB) say on the conference call for its trading update for the ten months to 31 Oct 2026, published on 18 Nov 2026, and how did each covering broker respond in the days that followed? The update (company announcement, 18 Nov 2026) reported organic revenue growth excluding InnovaMatrix of 6.6 percent for the ten months (5.0 percent in H1 2026); raised FY2026 organic growth guidance excluding InnovaMatrix to 6.25 to 7.0 percent from 5.5 to 6.5 percent; kept adjusted operating margin guidance of at least 23 percent, now including a currency headwind of about 65 basis points and an InnovaMatrix headwind of about 90 basis points; cut the currency benefit to revenue to about 90 basis points from 130; put capital expenditure at the upper end of $200m to $230m; cut equity cash conversion to about 90 percent from about 100 percent; and gave a cash tax rate of about 16 percent. Specifically: what growth did each segment (Infusion Care, Continence Care, Ostomy Care, Advanced Wound Care excluding InnovaMatrix) deliver in the four months July to October 2026, and how much of Infusion Care's acceleration did management attribute to the timing of customer orders, to higher output from existing lines and to underlying demand; what did management say about growth, comparatives and the timing of new Infusion Care capacity in the first and second halves of 2027; about input-cost inflation in 2027, the share of 2027 raw materials hedged, pricing measures in France and Germany and the proposed French levy on reimbursed medical devices; about why cash conversion guidance was reduced, expected leverage at 31 Dec 2026, and the timing and size of any share buyback after the $200m programme; and about the 2027 margin target? For each covering broker (including Bernstein, Deutsche Bank, DNB Carnegie, Jefferies, Morgan Stanley, Rothschild & Co Redburn, JP Morgan, Goldman Sachs, RBC, Barclays and Stifel): how did it change its FY2026 to FY2028 estimates for organic growth, revenue, segment revenue, adjusted operating margin and EPS, its rating and its target price; which valuation multiple does its target price apply; and what did it say about the shares after they rose about 9 percent on the day?

Request: OWN-2

Question: What adjusted operating margin guidance and organic revenue growth guidance did Convatec (CTEC-GB) give for each financial year from FY2019 to FY2026, at each event (full-year results, four-month trading update, half-year results, ten-month trading update), on which basis (constant currency or reported), and what did it deliver on the same basis? In particular: was the FY2022 margin guidance "at least 18.0 percent in constant currency" against a reported result of 19.5 percent, what was the FY2022 margin in constant currency, and how much of the reported rise from 17.7 percent in FY2021 was due to currency; was "at least 19.7 percent" the opening guidance for FY2023, later raised to at least 20.5 percent in constant currency, and what was the FY2023 result in constant currency against the reported 20.2 percent; and what were the FY2024 and FY2025 margin guidance and results on each basis? How did Convatec's adjusted gross margin, operating expenses and adjusted operating margin move between the first and second halves of each year from 2019 to 2025, and how did the cost inflation of 2022 and 2023 (8.6 percent in cost of goods sold in FY2022, per the company's Capital Markets Day of April 2026) pass into its gross margin? On which dates were the $300m share buyback of 2025 and the $200m share buyback of 2026 announced, started and completed; what was net debt to adjusted EBITDA at each half-year and year-end from 2023 to June 2026; and what has management said about the seasonality of working capital, cash flow and leverage between the first and second halves, and about the point in the year at which it decides capital returns?

Request: OWN-3

Question: At what forward price-to-earnings multiples has Convatec (CTEC-GB) traded in each phase of its history since its 2016 IPO, what was happening to its guidance, to consensus estimates and to regulatory risks in the periods when its multiple stood above 18 times and below 13 times forward earnings, and how has its valuation discount to Coloplast moved over that time (Jefferies has cited a historical Coloplast premium of about 70 percent and a five-year average of about 55 percent; JP Morgan on 21 Oct 2026 cited Coloplast at 18.3 times and Convatec at 13.8 times forecast 2027 earnings)? Which multiples and peer discounts do covering brokers apply in their current target prices and in their published bull and bear cases for Convatec (for example Morgan Stanley's risk-reward update of 7 Sep 2026, Jefferies' note of 23 Jul 2026 and DNB Carnegie's initiation of 22 Oct 2026)? For European medical-technology companies that re-rated from a trough multiple after a period of regulatory or execution worries (for example Getinge, Oxford Instruments, Coloplast, Smith & Nephew, Fresenius, Demant and Straumann): how far and how fast did the forward multiple recover, how much of the recovery was shared with the sector and how much was the company's own, how large were the consensus EPS upgrades that accompanied it, what marked the point at which the recovery stalled, and how long did such re-ratings last after guidance was first raised? What are investors and commentators (fund letters, investor conferences, the financial press and social media) saying about Convatec's valuation, about its position relative to Coloplast and about a long Convatec, short Coloplast position since the trading update of 18 Nov 2026?

Terms used in the answers

Hover over a dotted term on a computer to see it written out. On a phone, this list is the reference. Terms not listed are company, product or trial names, or terms the Investment Analyst explains where it uses them.

AGM
annual general meeting
CFO
chief financial officer
CMS
the Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services, the United States federal health insurer's agency
DNB
DNB Carnegie, a Nordic broker
EBIT
earnings before interest and tax (operating profit)
EBITA
earnings before interest, tax and amortisation
EBITDA
earnings before interest, tax, depreciation and amortisation
EPS
earnings per share
EUR
euro
FDA
the United States Food and Drug Administration
FX
foreign exchange (currency)
FY
fiscal year (FY2027 or FY27 is the company's fiscal year 2027; the company's year-end is in its reports)
H1
first half of the fiscal year
H2
second half of the fiscal year
M&A
mergers and acquisitions
MDR
the European Union's Medical Device Regulation
Q2
second quarter
Q3
third quarter
Q4
fourth quarter
R&D
research and development
ROIC
return on invested capital
TSR
total shareholder return
USD
United States dollar
